ParlaMint-AT_2011-04-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00103_d7e3318	Madam President. Dear Members of the Government! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! In presenting the new members of the Federal Government yesterday, I had the opportunity to present Austria's stability and key figures on unemployment, the labour market, but also the key figures on per capita GDP, per capita income, i.e. prosperity criteria, living conditions in a country. I do not want to repeat this, but I would just like to stress that we know that this is not a success of a few politicians, but rather, in our country, due to a combination of the framework conditions of the economy, the workers and, of course, of a government that places great emphasis on the fact that, in comparison with Europe, we also claim to be among the better or even among the best in terms of the stability of economic growth, economic power, prosperity, even in some areas. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] When we talk about stable finances, you will easily recognise, compared to the deficits with other EU countries, that we have decided on what we decided in our budget path and what we now have to decide in the Federal Financial Framework Act, but we can point out that in 2013 we will achieve a Maastricht deficit of less than 3% and that 2014 2.4 percent and 2015 will be planned for 2 percent. Now I know, of course, it is the task of the opposition to question this and to consider it far too little, but I would like to stress that this performance is only possible through a very strict and disciplined leadership in the field of finance and that the Minister of Finance has rightly pointed out the importance of complying with the requirements for the ministries. And, of course, there are also the requirements for reform, for a simplification of administration, for a simplification of bureaucracy, for the same or even better performance with fewer people, i.e. to achieve better efficiency, including, otherwise, that would mean that one would only cut across the areas and thus result in a deterioration in quality. Therefore, in this deficit path, the commitment ahead of us to achieve the same quality in the individual areas with fewer people is also to be read out. [[Abg. Kickl: You could have just started your government conversion!]] In comparison with other countries within the European Union in relation to the Maastricht deficit – and it is not only our German neighbour, who also achieves very committed targets in the deficit – that is not only Ireland or Greece, but also the United Kingdom, which plays a major role today, with a deficit of 10.4 percent, also Poland with 7.9 percent, our neighbor Slovakia and France with 7 percent or the Eurozone on average, at 6 percent. It is therefore quite comparable, given that it is not based on statements such as: Compared with countries like Greece, we are better!, we can reduce, but we have not only not to shyen the comparison, but also to actively further improve the values, also to those countries which are quite comparable from economic performance, from the possibilities. They are therefore committed goals. Not to be forgotten, of course, that debt can be assessed differently across Europe, because it is a difference whether one has debt in a country, for example, through the expansion of infrastructure. If everyone is in favour of expanding the infrastructure of a country, and it always plays a major role in all the speeches in all parties, that one should show consistency, persistence and reliability, for example, in expanding the rail, if one wants to do so, of course, one must know that this is also a matter of debt and that debt is not only due to the fact that one simply spends too much in the daily budget of a ministry, which is the worst case, but that, of course, there is a lot of investment in these figures, which, in the medium and long term, is a good infrastructure for the economic location of Austria, for environmental policy, for the accessibility of the regions in our country and offers opportunities. So I would like to say that in the EU-27 2010 there was a total debt of 80 percent, in the euro area – I am only talking about the average – a total debt of 85 percent, while in Austria it was 72.3 percent. Of course, this Austrian value can and must improve in the medium and long term, but I would like to stress that the accounting of debt, which is partly investment, has led Eurostat to these averages in the European Union, in the euro area. Why do I say that? – Because I am also convinced that these investments throughout Europe do not go by. It would be easy to say Polish: Let us halve that!, but we would not halve what some of them of which are superficially promising, namely some unnecessary expenditure that one deletes anyway, without noticing it, because they are part of the issue of efficiency improvement. No, we would delete essential areas of the future. Of course, some finance ministers, among the previous governments, have tried not to include such investments in the field of railways, for example, is legitimate from the point of view of the finance ministers, but that in the European Union, where the data truth, the reliability of facts has an ever greater role to play... – Yes, precisely from its teachings! [[Abg. Kickl: We have seen this with Greece and Portugal, the data truth!]] For example, if one says that in Greece one should have done much more clearly, harder, better, then it is of course no wonder that Eurostat and now the European Commission are very much insisting that these investments in debt should also be included. By the way, they were particularly creative under black and blue in the breakdowns from debt. Therefore, we do not want to dig so far in history, but rather deal with the future, and the future means that we know very well that it is a medium and long-term task to reduce this debt, but that this is not a task where one simply announces something, so that one would risk the essential foundations of the future, such as the infrastructure, but that this is a consistent, reliable, reliable work. In the field of education, research, art and culture, we have a plus of €700 million in 2010 to 2015, i.e. an increase of 6 per cent, in the field of internal security a plus of €180 million, i.e. an increase of 8 per cent, and in the field of infrastructure comes from this calculation of actual investments, as I have tried to explain from the principle, even a plus of over 60 per cent for this period, but also in the area of families a plus of €350 million is added – in total expenditure. I know that the detailed discussion, the discussion on the individual positions, has been very detailed here, but I am talking about the total expenditure for these years, and there is also an increase of 5.5 percent. Also in the field of school, education must be clear to us that meeting the requirement we ourselves have chosen, that we also want to be among the best in the educational comparisons in Europe, where we know that we are too far behind there and that we see an obligation to invest in school, in childcare, in universities, of course, means extra expenditure. There will always be too little, but additional expenditure is more expenditure, and additional expenditure, for example, to meet the demand for the expansion of the New Secondary School, 117 new locations or 80,000 new places in the whole day school area, shows that this task is not a theoretical task, but a practical one that is actually to be done by us and for which appropriate financial equipment is to be ensured. It is therefore important to save in the right place – so difficult that is and so controversial one can discuss it because: What's the right place? On the other hand, revenue is important, as we have decided together: EC internal market, EC internal market, free movement of goods, free movement of goods, free movement of goods, free I know that some – and I too – say that this is only a first step, but it is a step in the right direction in our country, and it would also be the financial transaction tax the right step in Europe. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Neugebauer.]] For the following position, Austria will always promote much commitment in European policy as a member country of the EU: If we say that social peace is a location factor, if we say that social balance in a country has something to do with living conditions in that country, then of course there must also be revenue from the state. And a competition in the European Union – who offers businesses the lowest taxes, who gives businesses the most to come to us – is an unpleasant competition, because it does not lead to our goal, but to the wrong goal of finally having the financial possibilities to invest in education, in research, in development. [[Abg. Kickl: They reward the evildoers! It's adventurous what you're trying to do!]] We must therefore reject dumping at European level both in the case of tax revenue and, of course, in the case of wage dumping. There are also in the European Union some worshipers of ideology: Wages down, living conditions, reducing social networks – then you can compete with Asia! No, ladies and gentlemen, we want a federal financial framework, we want a policy in Europe that does not mean reducing people's living conditions, reducing the welfare criteria, reducing wages! [[Abg. Kickl: That means no money to Ireland, isn't it?]] No, we want to achieve something together, with the creativity of the population, with the opportunities of workers, well equipped through education, development and research, so that the opportunity in competition can also be realized, so that the shared profits can be distributed fairly – for a socially just Austria and a socially just Europe!

This is generally regarded as a yellow light signal in the budgetary field and in the clear text means that we have to accept new debt in order to pay interest. There is another warning signal regarding the operation of the debt: We are now at a rate where, at a historically low interest rate, we will soon spend no less than €10 billion on interest rates, and that means at a budget volume of €70 billion, 15 percent. So 15 percent of the total government budget is interest. So we have structural budget problems, and they require structural answers. There, too, the criteria for a common monetary union were laid down. At the time, it was established that public debt should not exceed 60 percent of GDP and that re-indebtedness – the deficit – should not exceed 3 percent of GDP. Ladies and gentlemen! In the future, legal experts with special knowledge will be provided; they are available free of charge. I would like to focus briefly on an aspect that is very important to me personally, namely the field of education and German pre-accession. This is a legitimate claim. Third-country nationals who want to settle permanently in Austria must have basic German language skills before immigration. This is not an immeasurable or harassment, as it is partly presented. This is a matter of course in other countries and in Germany, for example, since 2007. This is not intended to make it possible to move into the social system, but to improve access to the labour market. Language skills are indeed the basic prerequisite for later successful integration and the basic prerequisite for successfully integrating themselves into the work process. Neither a doctor's visit nor a speech in school can be observed without German knowledge. German is undoubtedly a difficult but also a very beautiful language. German is the language of Goethe and Schiller, but also of Adalbert Stifter and Franz Grillparzer, to mention two Austrian examples. German is the native language of more than one hundred million citizens in Europe and of course of many thousands of German speakers in many countries of the world. Successful integration without language knowledge is not imaginable. The acquisition of the language is the gateway to a new, previously foreign world. [[Congratulations on MEPs of the ÖVP.]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Korun.]] – Mrs Korun, be quiet and be careful! Why the opposition is so vehemently opposed to language acquisition is really not understandable. [[New call of the abg. May. Korun.]] We live in a knowledge society, and we talk about life-long and lifelong learning. – Why should the legitimate, correct and important demand for the acquisition and learning of the German language be a problem now? ParlaMint-AT_2011-04-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00103_d7e13963	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! The Treaty with the Republic of Slovenia on the course of the State border deals with technical clarifications and new border documents relating to different border sections. The Convention on Police Cooperation in South-East Europe is an important further step in international cooperation. As the Republic of Austria, we already work closely with many countries. The Convention, which is now under way, regulates and establishes contractually good cooperation with countries such as Albania, Bosnia-Herzegovina, Macedonia, Serbia, Montenegro, Moldova, Bulgaria and Romania. Successful cross-border police cooperation is always evidenced by successes, such as the beating of the police about a year ago against a Georgian perpetrator group. The figures of crime statistics underline the good work of the Austrian police. The threat to our security, for example, is quite diverse by international terrorism, cross-border crime and illegal migration. By strengthening international cooperation, such challenges can best be met. I can also use this opportunity to draw attention to an example of international cooperation and successful Austrian police work from my constituency. For years, the Bad Kreuzen Service Dog Training Centre has been working with Central Asian states such as Kazakhstan, Uzbekistan, Turkmenistan and Tajikistan. On the initiative of the American Embassy and with the approval and support of the Austrian Ministry of the Interior, cooperation began years ago with the fact that Kazakh service dogs leaders in Austria were equipped and trained with special dogs. The dogs trained with Austrian help are used mainly in border use and against drug dealers and explosive offences in these central Asian states. These countries are located on drug routes from Afghanistan and China to Europe. With Austrian knowledge, crime is thus solved or prevented. In recent months five employees of the UN have been trained in Bad Kreuzen in the field of explosives. At the end of the course at the beginning of May, high-ranking representatives of the UN promised their arrival in Bad Kreuzen. Chief Inspector Josef Schützenhofer as the head of the Bad Kreuzen Service Dog Training Centre and his team can only be congratulated warmly for their successful and committed work, but above all I would like to thank them for it. Bad Kreuzen is a sign for the good work of the Austrian police. The market municipality of Bad Kreuzen and the country Oberösterreich support the service dog training centre as much as possible. Estimated Minister of the Interior Mag. Mikl leader, I'm looking for you... – Your message will be delivered already, don't worry about it! [[Call BZÖ: She's not here!]] – Like her predecessor, Dr. Maria Fekter, she will continue to promote and support the Bad Kreuzen Service Dog Training Centre in the future.

I would just like to draw attention to one change, namely that in the future, graduates of music universities will also have the opportunity to participate in a professional maturity test, and those who have completed training in the healing massage or the healing massage will also find this access. I think that should be mentioned. Another amendment which has not yet been tabled by my colleague and previous speaker, which I would therefore like to mention, is the amendment which is related to the preparation for the professional test, namely who is entitled to take part in it. So far, only graduates were entitled to do so. With this innovation, it will be possible to teach candidates who have a relevant technical degree, i.e. at least 12 months of professional experience as a lecturer in education, continuing education and training. I would like to mention this because I think it makes the semi-central Matura in all its facets a round, complete thing. I also believe that in total it will raise the level of this degree and, above all, constitute a quality criterion for it when it comes to being able to compare Matura at different schools in all the federal states and to know that the graduates can do much accordingly. The second point on the agenda is the Examining Tax Act. In principle, it is a continuation of the current system when it comes to paying teachers for the assessment of a Bachelor's thesis exam premiums. Here the status quo is simply continued and extended, and I assume that no consent can stand in the way. In this sense: Two measures to be supported. Thank you.

Grosz: The Minister of Finance!]] In such a case, an aircraft is considered a safety problem and the use of airspace surveillance aircraft is intended to identify and clarify the cause of the contact loss. The aircraft used for the identification of these unregistered aircrafts by the Austrian Federal Army intercept the unknown machines and lead them to the Austrian border. As you all know, pilots need a certain number of hours of flight to carry out their profession. This is, of course, particularly true of our federal army pilots. COMMLOSS operations are also suitable as practical exercises for our federal army pilots, and therefore one can estimate the financial loss in these cases a little less and also see something positive in these operations when they are considered to be training missions for our pilots. Although these COMMLOSS flights undoubtedly cause costs to the Federal Ministry, a calculation of the costs incurred can realistically only be regulated at the pan-European level in accordance with the 'polluter pays' principle. That's the problem we're having at the moment. At European level, this issue is not currently being pursued. An Airspace Security Conference took place in Brussels in 2008, where it was rated as No Option. [[Abg. Grosz: Just not too much!]] That's still the state of things.
